I don't know, I just keep messing up the execution somehow. But no, I can remember things normally.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel mentally tired after working on a project?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, editing a video can take a long time, but I don't finish a project and feel mentally exhausted or anything. My brain actually feels fine. I don't get that wiped-out feeling... I just kind of wrap up and move on to whatever is next. So, no, mental fatigue isn't really something I've noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself unable to think of the right thing to do in a situation, like your mind goes blank?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I've ever really had that happen. My mind doesn't go blank. I usually always know what I need to do next, especially when I'm working on a video or setting up a shot. I don't freeze up or... lose my train of thought like that. So, no, that's not really an issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're watching yourself do things, rather than actually doing them?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I've ever felt like that. I'm definitely the one doing the things... I don't feel disconnected or like I'm watching myself from the outside. I'm pretty present in whatever I'm doing, even if I'm rushing through it. So no, I haven't experienced that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not fully in control of your actions?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I know I can do things quickly or jump into something without thinking it all the way through, but I'm still the one making the choice to do it. I never feel like I've actually lost control of my body or what I'm doing. It's always me steering, even if I'm moving fast, so... no, I haven't really felt that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're doing things on autopilot?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't think I zone out like that. With my work, you know, operating the camera or going through footage, I have to be actively paying attention to what's right in front of me. I might rush through things sometimes, but I'm still fully awake and... making the decisions as I go. So, no, I wouldn't say I ever really feel like I'm just on autopilot.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. So, no, that's not really an issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're watching yourself do things, rather than actually doing them?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I've ever felt like that. I don't ever really feel distant or detached from where I am.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're in a daze or fog while working on a project?\n\nPatient: No, I don't get that. I don't feel like I'm outside my body watching myself or anything like that. Or I'll export a client cut with the wrong lower-third graphic, even after I've already checked it. It's just getting to be a problem, so I needed to see someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I get pretty frustrated with myself when I mess up a project, but... no, I wouldn't say I feel sad or down. My mood is mostly fine, it's really just the work mistakes that are stressing me out.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble focusing or paying attention to your work lately?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I can focus on my work just fine. When I'm sitting at my editing bay, I'm paying attention to what I'm doing. It doesn't feel like my mind is wandering off or anything like that. I just... I don't know, I just keep making those mistakes anyway. But the actual focus part seems okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or nervous, either in general or in specific situations?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I get stressed out when I realize I've messed up a client's video or something, but I wouldn't call it anxiety. I don't feel nervous in general, and I'm not really worried about things until after I make a mistake. So, no, anxiety isn't really an issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things that you normally would be able to recall?\n\nPatient: No, my memory is fine. I don't really have any issues remembering things. I know what I'm supposed to be doing and I remember all the steps for editing and everything. It's not a memory problem, it's just... I don't know, I just keep messing up the execution somehow. But no, I can remember things normally.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel mentally tired after working on a project?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, editing a video can take a long time, but I don't finish a project and feel mentally exhausted or anything. My brain actually feels fine. I don't get that wiped-out feeling... I just kind of wrap up and move on to whatever is next. So, no, mental fatigue isn't really something I've noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself unable to think of the right thing to do in a situation, like your mind goes blank?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I've ever really had that happen. My mind doesn't go blank. I usually always know what I need to do next, especially when I'm working on a video or setting up a shot. I don't freeze up or... lose my train of thought like that. So, no, that's not really an issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're watching yourself do things, rather than actually doing them?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I've ever felt like that. I'm definitely the one doing the things... I don't feel disconnected or like I'm watching myself from the outside. I'm pretty present in whatever I'm doing, even if I'm rushing through it. So no, I haven't experienced that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not fully in control of your actions?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I know I can do things quickly or jump into something without thinking it all the way through, but I'm still the one making the choice to do it. I never feel like I've actually lost control of my body or what I'm doing. It's always me steering, even if I'm moving fast, so... no, I haven't really felt that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're doing things on autopilot?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't think I zone out like that. With my work, you know, operating the camera or going through footage, I have to be actively paying attention to what's right in front of me. I might rush through things sometimes, but I'm still fully awake and... making the decisions as I go. So, no, I wouldn't say I ever really feel like I'm just on autopilot.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're distant from yourself or your surroundings while working on a project?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. When I'm on a project, I'm actually really locked into what's going on. If I'm behind the camera, I have to be totally aware of my surroundings to get the shot, so I'm very grounded in the environment. I don't ever really feel distant or detached from where I am.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're in a daze or fog while working on a project?\n\nPatient: No, I don't get that. Um, I don't really experience any kind of brain fog or anything like that. When I'm working, I have to be pretty dialed in to what's happening. I might be rushing around or, you know, doing things a bit too fast, but my head is clear. So no, no daze or fog.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're outside of your body watching yourself make those technical mistakes at work?\n\nPatient: No, I've never had that happen. I don't feel like I'm outside my body watching myself or anything like that. When I mess up a shot or make a mistake editing, I'm very much in my own head—usually just annoyed at myself for moving too fast. But I'm definitely right there in my body the whole time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're not yourself when you're making those technical mistakes at work?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I know it's me making the mistakes. I don't feel like a different person or anything. It's just me being impatient or not paying close enough attention to the details, but I always feel like myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're doing things without being fully aware of why you're doing them?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I might do things on impulse sometimes—like making a sudden adjustment to the camera or jumping into an edit without planning it out—but I always know what I'm doing and why I'm doing it in the moment. I don't just act without being aware of it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don't ever really feel distant or detached from where I am.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're in a daze or fog while working on a project?\n\nPatient: No, I don't get that. Um, I don't really experience any kind of brain fog or anything like that. When I'm working, I have to be pretty dialed in to what's happening. I might be rushing around or, you know, doing things a bit too fast, but my head is clear. So no, no daze or fog.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're outside of your body watching yourself make those technical mistakes at work?\n\nPatient: No, I've never had that happen. I don't feel like I'm outside my body watching myself or anything like that. When I mess up a shot or make a mistake editing, I'm very much in my own head—usually just annoyed at myself for moving too fast. But I'm definitely right there in my body the whole time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're not yourself when you're making those technical mistakes at work?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I know it's me making the mistakes. I don't feel like a different person or anything. It's just me being impatient or not paying close enough attention to the details, but I always feel like myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're doing things without being fully aware of why you're doing them?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I might do things on impulse sometimes—like making a sudden adjustment to the camera or jumping into an edit without planning it out—but I always know what I'm doing and why I'm doing it in the moment. I don't just act without being aware of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're in a dream or everything around you seems unreal while you're working?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I haven't ever experienced anything like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. But the actual focus part seems okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or nervous, either in general or in specific situations?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I get pretty frustrated with myself when I mess up a project, but... no, I wouldn't say I feel sad or down. My mood is mostly fine, it's really just the work mistakes that are stressing me out.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble focusing or paying attention to your work lately?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I can focus on my work just fine. When I'm sitting at my editing bay, I'm paying attention to what I'm doing. It doesn't feel like my mind is wandering off or anything like that. I just... I don't know, I just keep making those mistakes anyway. But the actual focus part seems okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or nervous, either in general or in specific situations?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I get stressed out when I realize I've messed up a client's video or something, but I wouldn't call it anxiety. I don't feel nervous in general, and I'm not really worried about things until after I make a mistake. So, no, anxiety isn't really an issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble remembering things that you normally would be able to recall?\n\nPatient: No, my memory is fine. I don't really have any issues remembering things. I know what I'm supposed to be doing and I remember all the steps for editing and everything. It's not a memory problem, it's just... I don't know, I just keep messing up the execution somehow. But no, I can remember things normally.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel mentally tired after working on a project?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, editing a video can take a long time, but I don't finish a project and feel mentally exhausted or anything. My brain actually feels fine. I don't get that wiped-out feeling... I just kind of wrap up and move on to whatever is next. So, no, mental fatigue isn't really something I've noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself unable to think of the right thing to do in a situation, like your mind goes blank?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I've ever really had that happen. My mind doesn't go blank. I usually always know what I need to do next, especially when I'm working on a video or setting up a shot. I don't freeze up or... lose my train of thought like that. So, no, that's not really an issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're watching yourself do things, rather than actually doing them?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I've ever felt like that. I'm definitely the one doing the things... I don't feel disconnected or like I'm watching myself from the outside. I'm pretty present in whatever I'm doing, even if I'm rushing through it. So no, I haven't experienced that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not fully in control of your actions?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I know I can do things quickly or jump into something without thinking it all the way through, but I'm still the one making the choice to do it. I never feel like I've actually lost control of my body or what I'm doing. It's always me steering, even if I'm moving fast, so... no, I haven't really felt that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're doing things on autopilot?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't think I zone out like that. With my work, you know, operating the camera or going through footage, I have to be actively paying attention to what's right in front of me. I might rush through things sometimes, but I'm still fully awake and... making the decisions as I go. So, no, I wouldn't say I ever really feel like I'm just on autopilot.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're distant from yourself or your surroundings while working on a project?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. I don't ever really feel distant or detached from where I am.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're in a daze or fog while working on a project?\n\nPatient: No, I don't get that. Um, I don't really experience any kind of brain fog or anything like that. When I'm working, I have to be pretty dialed in to what's happening. I might be rushing around or, you know, doing things a bit too fast, but my head is clear. So no, no daze or fog.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're outside of your body watching yourself make those technical mistakes at work?\n\nPatient: No, I've never had that happen. I don't feel like I'm outside my body watching myself or anything like that. But I'm definitely right there in my body the whole time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're not yourself when you're making those technical mistakes at work?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. So, no, that's not really an issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're watching yourself do things, rather than actually doing them?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I've ever felt like that. I just kind of wrap up and move on to whatever is next. So, no, that's not really an issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're watching yourself do things, rather than actually doing them?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I've ever felt like that. So no, I haven't experienced that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not fully in control of your actions?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I never feel like I've actually lost control of my body or what I'm doing. It's always me steering, even if I'm moving fast, so... no, I haven't really felt that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're doing things on autopilot?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't think I zone out like that. With my work, you know, operating the camera or going through footage, I have to be actively paying attention to what's right in front of me. So no, no daze or fog.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're outside of your body watching yourself make those technical mistakes at work?\n\nPatient: No, I've never had that happen. I don't feel like I'm outside my body watching myself or anything like that.
